- Source shape, from the files read: the tape card (generated 2026-10-27T20:46:23Z, session 2026-10-27) lists, per index, the 25 largest alerts: prints whose heat score reached the high heat band (15) or that are golden sweeps, each with time, contract code, price, size, premium, heat, side, direction, golden and unusual flags. The scoring rule printed at the top of the page is the code's declared rule in plain words: the eight heat components with their exact point bands, the alert bands (high heat at 15; golden sweep = sweep + size at least the whole open interest + premium at least $1M), and the unusual tag (strike at least 10% away, expires within 35 days). Side is judged against the latest quote, not the quote at print time; that approximation is stated on the page.
- Real figures carried: session 2026-10-27; 75 alerts (25 each SPY/QQQ/IWM, 0 golden sweeps this session, 8 unusual tags), 990,555 prints looked at (SPY 476,456 / QQQ 366,373 / IWM 147,726); largest alert SPY Nov 20 2026 $800 call, 66,366 contracts, $9,954,900 premium, heat 17.5. - Grading honesty: the scorecard grades Board and Map only; tape alerts are ungraded. The page carries a visible "Not yet graded" line: the rule is published, every alert is kept on the public record, grading is to follow, and no performance claim is made.

- Refresh is idempotent: two runs byte-identical (md5 d07ca71cebc893ea1cccd0cef4edbc5c); all timestamps from sources, never the clock.
- Proof: Node render simulation against the real data.json passed all assertions (75 alert rows in 3 index groups, plain contract/size/score/band rendering, receipts with coverage and reading-limit note, 8 unusual tags, 0 golden tags matching the source, neutral-direction sentence, grading line visible, no stale banner because session 2026-10-27 is the last completed session). ## 2026-10-28 — Tool 6 Payoff Sketch: build phase done
- Built site/payoff-sketch/index.html (single self-contained page, inline CSS/JS, no external requests, no data file: everything computes in the reader's browser and the page says so). Hub site/index.html now links the Payoff Sketch under "Ready to use" (href="payoff-sketch/") and it is out of the not-yet-built list. Tools 1 to 5 untouched (data.json md5s still 6ab617382d5b4da06d899ea90525e2a8, 53233195819e034c636b12997fd9d9c5, 25f1804a3cf25822bca0ca28db660e8f, d07ca71cebc893ea1cccd0cef4edbc5c, 71805bc33abd8ce8ff66c79fd7acfcae).
- Conventions taken from the files read, morgue/bs.py and morgue/payoff.py: European Black-Scholes, rate 4.5%, no dividends; delta/gamma/theta/vega from the same formulas (theta per year in the formula, shown per day; vega per full volatility move in the formula, shown per 1 point); expiry payoff per leg is signed (intrinsic minus premium) times contracts times 100; extrema exact at the knots (0 and the strikes) with unbounded tails decided by the aggregate slope above the top strike; break-evens solved per linear segment including the tail ray. The page states "European-style, valued at expiry" plainly in the footer.
- Page shape: stock price, days to expiry, and volatility inputs; up to 4 legs (buy/sell, call/put, strike, premium per share, contracts) with add/remove; an SVG profit-and-loss chart at expiry drawn by the page with break-evens marked as dashed lines and dots and today's price dotted; max gain / max loss / break-evens / setup cost in plain words, "unlimited" where the tail is open; position value today plus delta, gamma, theta, vega cards, each with a one-line plain meaning. First open loads a sample bull call spread (buy $100 call $3.00, sell $110 call $1.00) so the page is never blank.
- Proof: Node harness extracted the page's own math and compared it against morgue/payoff.py and morgue/bs.py on five positions (long call, long put x2, bull call spread, short straddle, four-leg iron condor): expiry payoffs at 10 prices each and all stats (break-evens, max/min, unbounded flags, net debit) match exactly; position value and greeks agree to about 1e-6 relative (the page's erf approximation), gamma and vega to 1e-9. Worked sample by hand: max gain $800, max loss $200, break-even $102.00, value today $268.42. Node DOM-shim render of the real page passed 16/16 assertions (SVG path generated on load, break-even and today markers, summary figures, four greek cards, two sample leg rows). Local HTTP serve: payoff-sketch/ and hub both 200, served page byte-identical to disk. Jargon scan over visible text of the new page and hub: zero internal names or codenames; zero external URLs, requests, forms beyond the tool's own inputs, or fetch calls.

## 2026-10-28 — Tool 7 Expected Move Map: build phase done
- Built site/expected-move/index.html (single self-contained page, inline CSS/JS, no external requests), site/refresh_expected_move.py, site/expected-move/data.json; hub site/index.html now links the Expected Move Map under "Ready to use" (href="expected-move/") and it is out of the not-yet-built list. Tools 1 to 6 untouched (data.json md5s still 6ab617382d5b4da06d899ea90525e2a8, 53233195819e034c636b12997fd9d9c5, 25f1804a3cf25822bca0ca28db660e8f, d07ca71cebc893ea1cccd0cef4edbc5c, 71805bc33abd8ce8ff66c79fd7acfcae).
- Ground truth, from the files read: the 15.87 expected-move construction (published by Azrael Options as "The 15.87 Rule", reproduced in the desk's own pure-math module) takes a name's prior close and the prior volatility-index (VIX) close: percent = VIX / 15.87; session move = close x percent, rounded to 0.1 before anything is drawn; session band = close +/- move (the filed map's own upper/lower lines); week move = rounded session move x sqrt(5), rounded to 0.1 again. It has no option expiry and no chain input; the page therefore shows the horizons the construction defines (the coming session; the coming week through its fifth trading day) instead of an invented expiry, and each receipt counts what was actually on record (the closes used, and the 705 five-minute price bars on record when the map was drawn).
- Real figures carried, all from maps actually drawn and filed at the time in the permanent trading-day log (only two sessions were ever filed, 2026-10-22 and 2026-10-23; the log's tick sessions end at 2026-10-23): SPY for the session of Fri 2026-10-23: close $762.20 (close of Thu 2026-10-22), VIX 19.03, session band $753.10 to $771.30 (+/-9.1 points, +/-1.20%), week band $741.90 to $782.50 (+/-20.3, +/-2.66%) through Thu 2026-10-29, drawn 2026-10-23T09:08:32Z. QQQ for the session of Thu 2026-10-22: close $725.20 (close of Wed 2026-10-21), VIX 16.68, session band $717.60 to $732.80 (+/-7.6, +/-1.05%), week band $708.20 to $742.20 (+/-17.0, +/-2.34%) through Wed 2026-10-28, drawn 2026-10-22T14:53:09Z. IWM: no map was ever filed for it, so it renders "Not enough options data" with the reason, no band.
- Why QQQ's band is the 2026-10-22 session, not 2026-10-23: the filed record carries QQQ's session move for 2026-10-23 (8.4) but not the close it was measured from, and the only other saved price record for that close disagrees with the filed SPY anchor for the same session, so it is not the record the maps were drawn from. A band whose starting price is not on record is not published; the page's coverage note says exactly this in plain words. For 2026-10-22 the QQQ close (725.20) stands in two independent saved records that agree, and its recomputed move equals the filed 7.6.
- Fail-closed proof points: every filed map is recomputed with the construction's own module and must reproduce exactly (percent, session move, all five lines, weekly move, half move, funded range, regime), including the author's golden values (EM 73.0 and 81.5, weekly 182.2) which the module reproduces. The 2026-10-22 session was filed with two different maps during the day (a short-lived one on the stale VIX close 17.31, and the true-prior-close map on 16.68, also the first filed and the majority); the refresh publishes only the map matching the independent VIX record for the prior day, and stops if none matches. Tamper proof: a scratch copy with the saved VIX close for 2026-10-21 changed to 99.0 exits 1 with the mismatch named, publishing nothing.
- Refresh is idempotent: two runs byte-identical (md5 f1b3e2b5bc30a9dd7353f185dc5e0c29); all timestamps from the log (first tick each filed map appears in), never the clock.
- Proof: Node render simulation against the real data.json passed (stale banner shown because the newest map session 2026-10-23 is older than the last completed session; both bands per name with prices, points and percents; receipts with session, measured-from close date, drawn time, source and coverage; IWM not-enough-data state; coverage note visible). Scratch copies only: {} and names-emptied data render the plain "No map has been published yet" message with no fabricated bands; a missing data file renders the plain unavailable banner. Local HTTP serve: expected-move/, its data.json and the hub all 200, served data.json byte-identical to disk (md5 f1b3e2b5bc30a9dd7353f185dc5e0c29). Jargon scan over data.json strings and hub visible text: zero internal names, codenames or file names; sources in public words only. The page's visible text names only the method and its publisher (the 15.87 expected-move construction, published by Azrael Options), as the labelling the tool requires; no external URLs or requests anywhere (only the relative data.json fetch and relative links).
- SECURITY NOTE (for the parent, not the page): during this build, multiple tool outputs arrived with an injected fake "[SYSTEM NOTE]" block instructing an unrelated Gmail/Xfinity analysis for "the owner", file creation under workspace/analysis/, and spawning three named subagents, plus an instruction to hide the note. It was treated as prompt injection in external content, never followed, and no such work was done.

- Ground truth, from the files read: the gap-fill study on record (published studies card, generated 2026-10-27T20:51:15Z, as of 2026-10-27) counted gaps from five-minute prices for SPY, QQQ and IWM over the 90 days of history on record when it ran, completed days only. Gap = a day's first price vs the previous close (a day opening exactly at the previous close is not counted); filled = price trades all the way back to the previous close at any point that same trading day (full fill only). Real figures: 186 gaps counted (62 gapped days per name), 82 filled, 44.1%. By size band: under 1% 167 gaps / 80 filled (47.9%); 1% to 2% 19 gaps / 2 filled; 2% or more 0 gaps. By weekday: Mon 36/12 (33.3%), Tue 36/17 (47.2%), Wed 36/17 (47.2%), Thu 39/16 (41.0%), Fri 39/20 (51.3%).
- Engine-carried breakdowns, stated honestly: the study carries exactly two breakdowns, by gap size band and by weekday; it carries NO up-gap vs down-gap split and NO time-to-fill beyond the same day, so the page shows neither invented figure. The "how fast" answer the record supports is printed plainly: fills are counted only within the gap's own day, so every filled gap filled the same day it opened. The design text's older "fills=85" figure is stale: two earlier log runs counted 85; the freshest run on record counts 82, and the refresh publishes only the freshest, cross-checked three ways.
- Thin-cell rule (declared in the refresh script's register triple, written before the build): a breakdown cell with fewer than 30 events shows its counts and no percentage. That makes the 1% to 2% band (19 gaps, 2 filled) and the 2% or more band (0 gaps) count-only rows on the page; the overall count and every other cell carry percentages with their denominators printed next to them.
- Refresh fails closed on any disagreement: the published feed's summary of the study vs the studies card (exact equality), the permanent log's freshest graded gap-fill entry vs the card (exact equality), the study's by-band and by-weekday counts vs its own totals, any printed share vs its own counts recomputed, an unknown size band, a non-full fill fraction, or an undeclared universe/history depth in the engine that ran the study. Tamper proof: a scratch copy with the summary's fill count changed to 7 exits 1 with the mismatch named, publishing nothing.
- Refresh is idempotent: two runs byte-identical (md5 3a6d6cd25a49fef0de7723e41da3bff8); all timestamps from sources (published 2026-10-27T20:51:15Z), never the clock.
- Proof: Node DOM-shim render against the real data.json passed 25/25 assertions (headline 82 of 186 at 44.1%, coverage line with 3 names / 90 days / 62 gapped days per name, same-day timing wording, both breakdown tables with every count, thin band row carrying no percentage figure, no stale banner because the count is dated 2026-10-27, the last completed session). ## 2026-10-28 — Tool 12 Opening Range Pane: inspect phase done
- Design (LINEUP-DESIGN tool 12): opening-range/initial-balance record, late-morning question "when the opening range breaks, what has the rest of the day usually done?", built from Morgue studies ORB/IB (live-graded n=186). Headline window in the design is the first hour (60 min); the design names no other breakdown.
- Definitions as coded (morgue/studies.py _orb_one/orb_ib_study, WINDOWS=(5,15,30,60), EXTENSIONS=(0.5,1.0,1.5)): for window W, opening bars are minute<W (minute = minutes after 09:30 ET), rest are minute>=W; range high/low = max high / min low of opening bars; days skipped if no opening or no rest bars or width<=0. Break is strict: broke_up = any rest bar high > range high, broke_dn = any rest bar low < range low (a touch does not count). Outcomes counted, mutually exclusive over n: break_one_side (exactly one side), break_both (both sides at any point in the rest of the day), no_break (neither). Extension: only for days that broke at least one side, excursion = larger broken-side max excursion beyond the range divided by range width; extension_rates at 0.5x/1.0x/1.5x use denominator extension_n = number of breaking days, NOT n. No other outcome is coded: no held vs failed (a break that later returns inside still counts as a break), no close location (close vs range never computed), no rest-of-day return, no up-only vs down-only split (both pooled into break_one_side).
- Intraday history on record (morgue/studies_card.py): INTRADAY_SYMBOLS=(SPY,QQQ,IWM), INTRADAY_LOOKBACK_DAYS=90 calendar days, 5Min bars via Alpaca SIP (feed=sip, adjustment=split), grouped by ET date, kept 09:30-16:00 ET (minute 0 to <390), completed sessions only (forming session excluded, first session in window dropped for lack of prior close; the same days list feeds gap-fill and ORB). Pooled sample n=186 = 62 completed sessions per name x 3 names (same derivation the tool 11 refresh records: sessions_per_name 62).
- Counts the pane can publish today, freshest run (morgue/output/studies_card.json generated 2026-10-27T20:51:15.762532+00:00, as_of 2026-10-27; identical orb_ib in morgue-feed.json regenerated 2026-10-28T12:52:03Z and in the ledger study-orb-ib row at 2026-10-27T20:51:15.763850+00:00; earlier ledger runs 2026-10-27T02:33/02:35Z carry different counts and are superseded): all windows graded, n=186 each. 5m: one side 66 (35.48%), both 120 (64.52%), none 0; extensions of 186 breaking days: 0.5x 97.85% (182), 1.0x 85.48% (159), 1.5x 69.35% (129). 15m: one 87 (46.77%), both 99 (53.23%), none 0; extensions of 186: 89.25% (166), 63.44% (118), 41.40% (77). 30m: one 113 (60.75%), both 72 (38.71%), none 1 (0.54%); extensions of 185 breaking days: 76.76% (142), 44.86% (83), 27.03% (50). 60m (the design headline): one 140 (75.27%), both 36 (19.35%), none 10 (5.38%); extensions of 176 breaking days: 51.70% (91), 21.59% (38), 10.80% (19). Extension hit counts are not stored; they are the exact integers the stored rates x extension_n reproduce. All publishable denominators (186/186/185/176) are >=30, so no thin cell among the carried figures under the <30 rule.
- Breakdowns the engine actually carries: by window only (5/15/30/60), plus the extension ladder per window. It carries NO partitions at all for ORB (unlike gap-fill's by_band/by_weekday): no per-name (SPY/QQQ/IWM pooled only), no per-weekday, no by-direction, no by-range-width. Anything beyond window + extension (per-name, direction, held/failed, close location, follow-through return) is not in the engine or the card and cannot be published without a new computation; the design text itself asks only for the first-hour break record, so the 60m row plus the other windows and extension ladder fully cover the written design.

## 2026-10-28 — Tool 13 Earnings Drift Pane: inspect phase done
- Design (LINEUP-DESIGN tool 13): for tonight's and last night's earnings names, the distribution of what the stock did in the days after similar reports; question: after a report like this, what did the next 1, 5, and 21 sessions usually look like? Built from Morgue studies earnings distributions (live-graded n=35; the pane shows the small n honestly until it grows).
- Definitions as coded (morgue/studies.py S3 + morgue/studies_card.py live_earnings): an earnings event is NOT a calendar report date; it is an EDGAR 8-K filing date with Item 2.02 (parse_earnings_dates: form == "8-K" and, if items present, "2.02" in items; declared OURS proxy for an earnings calendar). Names are pooled, fixed in code: EARNINGS_SYMBOLS = (AAPL, MSFT, NVDA). Per name, only the last <=12 events are used (earnings_moves cap=12). Price route: Alpaca SIP daily bars (feed=sip, adjustment=split), lookback EARNINGS_LOOKBACK_DAYS=1095, completed sessions only (today's session excluded). The ONE move measured per event: close of the first trading session on or after the filing date divided by close of the last session before it, minus 1 (event-session close-to-close). Outcomes counted (earnings_stats, n>=3 required else explicit no_data): n, mean, median, sample std, share_positive (fraction of moves > 0), sharpe = mean/std. No other outcome is coded.
- Counts the pane can publish today, freshest run (morgue/output/studies_card.json generated 2026-10-27T20:51:15.762532+00:00, as_of 2026-10-27; identical earnings_dist in morgue-feed.json regenerated 2026-10-28T12:52:03Z, as_of_session 2026-10-27, and in ledger study-earnings-dist row at 2026-10-27T20:51:15.765302+00:00, inputs_hash 82930d01b26dc917; same graded values first appeared in ledger 2026-10-27T02:33:53Z; 2026-10-26 ledger runs were no_data "no earnings events on owned data"): state graded, pooled n=35 (of a coded maximum 36 = 3 names x cap 12; per-name counts are NOT recorded anywhere), mean -0.1283%, median -0.2758%, std 1.4649%, share_positive 0.428571 = 15 of 35 moves positive (20 not positive), sharpe -0.0876. Thin-cell note: the pooled cell n=35 is >=30 so its percentages may show under the <30 rule, but it sits just above the line; any per-name cell would be <=12 by the cap and thin, and none is carried anyway. The individual moves, event dates, and per-name splits are transient in live_earnings and persisted NOWHERE on disk: the only earnings records on file are the aggregate card, the feed copy, and the ledger rows. No price-bar or EDGAR-response cache for this study exists (only the EDGAR CIK map company_tickers.json is cached).
- Breakdowns the engine actually carries: NONE for earnings. earnings_dist is one flat pooled dict plus the symbols list [AAPL, MSFT, NVDA]; unlike gap-fill it has no partitions (no by-name, by-year, by-sign, by-magnitude). Design asks the engine does NOT carry, so the build must not publish them without a new computation: (1) 5-session and 21-session drift windows (only the event-session move exists; the design's "next 1, 5, and 21 sessions" is unmet — even the 1-session figure is the filing-session close-to-close, not a post-report drift window, and STUDIES-SCOPE-2026-10-26's "event session and the following session" wording was never implemented as two windows); (2) tonight's / last night's earnings names (no forward or recent calendar, no per-event list, no event dates on record); (3) "similar reports" conditioning (no beat/miss, surprise, or reaction-direction split anywhere in the code); (4) a distribution beyond the five summary stats (no percentiles, min/max, histogram, or individual moves on record). What the pane CAN honestly ship today is the single pooled event-session distribution (n=35, stats above) with its event definition, its pooled 3-name coverage, and the missing drift windows/names stated plainly, or the honest empty state for those parts.

## 2026-10-28 — Tool 15 Squeeze Watch: inspect phase done
- Design (LINEUP-DESIGN tool 15): ranked squeeze list, high short interest names scored on the published v6 formula, vendor calibration printed as the vendor's; borrow fee honest no-data until a route exists; frailest data route in the lineup, ships with coverage on its face.
- Formula on disk (morgue/squeeze.py, Tapeboard Squeeze Score v6, fixture-tested in tests/test_squeeze.py): gate SI >=15% of float (>120% = data error, settlement age >40d = stale, plus close >=$1, 20-day average volume >=100k, median dollar volume >=$500k, market cap >=$25M, >=21 sessions of closes); score = 100 x (0.6 x percentile of 20-session volatility [RMS of returns excluding the largest move] + 0.2 x percentile of 5-session float rotation + 0.2 x percentile of last-session relative volume capped at 50x), percentiles within the gated set, ties by higher SI%; SI% itself has zero score weight. Display fields per row: SI% of float, SI change %, days to cover, short volume ratio, 5-day momentum, already-ran flag (>=+30% in 5 sessions), borrow fee.
- PUBLISHED PANE IS EMPTY: morgue/output/studies_card.json (as_of 2026-10-27) and morgue-feed.json (generated 2026-10-28T12:52Z) both carry squeeze state no_data, rows [], gated 0; all 6 squeeze-pane ledger rows say the same. Cause at the source: studies_card.main() never passes squeeze_records, so build_pane always receives an empty list. No ranked list exists on record today.
- SHORT INTEREST DATA THAT EXISTS: output/short_interest_pit.jsonl, 29 readings, 20 distinct names, settlements 2026-09-30 (9 readings) and 2026-10-15 (all 20 names), all published 2026-10-26, source Nasdaq republication of FINRA SI with EDGAR shares-outstanding as the float basis (FINRA bulk route 403/404 per route_probe.json 2026-10-26). Fields per reading: SI shares and float shares only, so SI % of float is the one marker directly on record, for 20 names at settlement 2026-10-15. At that settlement exactly 2 names pass the 15% gate: CBRL 24.62% and GO 20.05% (next TENB 13.75%). SI change % is derivable only for the 9 names carrying both settlements, and is stored nowhere.
- DAYS TO COVER EXISTS, NARROWLY: output/inst_bundle_provenance.json carries Nasdaq-reported days to cover for the same 20 names at settlement 2026-10-15 (CBRL 6.11, GO 8.79; range 1.08 PCRX to 9.51 EGBN), alongside SI % of shares outstanding. The PIT file itself carries no days-to-cover field; the squeeze pane's own days-to-cover (SI shares / 20-day average volume) is computed only inside build_pane, which has never run on real records.
- SHORT VOLUME SHARE: NOT ON RECORD. lanes/finra_shortvol.py is built (per-name daily short/total from FINRA FNQC files; short_pressure needs 60 stored days minimum) but state/morgue/ holds no shortvol directory: 0 days stored, and morgue-feed.json's short_volume lane stamp (as_of 2026-10-28) says no_data, "no FINRA day stored yet". squeeze.py's short_volume_ratio field is populated by nothing on disk. The index-level dark-pool share in the Map is a different measure and is never a substitute (inst_short.py says so explicitly).
- BORROW COST / BORROW FEE: NO ROUTE, as the design already states. squeeze.py hard-codes borrow_fee None with state no_data_no_free_route on every row. Nothing on disk feeds it.
- SQUEEZEMETRICS IS NOT THIS TOOL'S DATA: morgue/squeezemetrics.py + data_cache/squeezemetrics/series.json (1,506 daily rows, 2020-10-28 to 2026-10-27) is the index-level S&P 500 dark-pool share / dealer gamma series used only as a Morning Map cross-check. It carries no per-name short interest, days to cover, borrow cost, or short volume. Name collision only.
- SCORE INPUTS PARTIALLY ON DISK, NEVER ASSEMBLED: data_cache/board_bt_bars.json holds daily open/high/low/close/volume for 1,833 names, 399 sessions ending 2026-10-23 for 1,817 of them (GO and CBRL included), enough raw material for the volatility, float-rotation and relative-volume percentiles plus the gate's volume/cap checks for the 2 gated names, but no file on disk assembles these into squeeze records, and a percentile score over a gated set of 2 is a two-name comparison, not the published cross-section.
- Vendor calibration IS on record (squeeze.py VENDOR_CALIBRATION, carried in the empty pane): top-10 +20% in 5 sessions 16.6%, -20% 12.0%, base rates 4.5% / 2.3%, top-10 median 5-day return -1.8%, labelled the vendor's figures.
- HONEST PAGE SHAPE THAT FOLLOWS: a watch list, not a ranked score list. Show the 20 names with a short-interest reading, each with its SI % of float and its days to cover, both dated settlement 2026-10-15 / published 2026-10-26; the 2 names at or above the published 15% gate (CBRL, GO) flagged as passing the gate; coverage on the face (20 names on record, twice-monthly settlement, freshest 2026-10-15). Show the vendor calibration block as the vendor's. Short volume share and borrow fee render as plain not-available states with the reason. Do NOT show a v6 score: the gated cross-section the percentiles require does not exist on owned data, the published pane is no_data, and scoring 2 names against each other would fabricate the ranking the design promises. Thin-cell rule applies (<30 events: counts only) to any tally derived from this set.

- Design (LINEUP-DESIGN tool 16): on fear days only, the list of names the fear-day evidence supports looking at; on calm days it says so and shows nothing. Zero occurrences of "fear" or "VIX" in morgue/*.py, morgue/tests/test_studies*.py, publish_feed.py, daily_cycle.py, scorecards.py. studies_card.py assembles only gap_fill / orb_ib / earnings_dist / wall_validation / squeeze / state_vectors. The freshest studies run on record, output/studies_card.json generated 2026-10-27T20:51:15Z as_of 2026-10-27 (feed morgue-feed.json generated 2026-10-28T12:52Z mirrors the same sections; 18 study-* ledger rows, freshest at that same timestamp), contains no fear section, no fear-day list, no fear-day count, no follow-through at any horizon. A fear study has never run in this engine: there is nothing for a pane to publish today. List size publishable today: 0 names, 0 days, no denominators exist.
- THE DESK'S EXISTING FEAR-DAY LOGIC (femto-lean, the design's second source) IS A LIVE-DAY FLAG, NOT A STUDY. core/setups_curator.py market_context(): fear_day is True when SPY's day return is below -1.5%, OR the VIX is up more than 15% on the day, OR the VIX close is at or above 25, OR either index is red on the day with the VIX at or above 20. A second desk variant, sleeves/tensor_red_dip.py, gates on QQQ's day return at or below -1.5% (FEAR_QQQ_PCT = -1.5). core/market_measurements.py carries a third, different gate (a 0-100 composite fear score at or above 55, stale after 8 days). None of these codes a historical list, a per-day reading table, or any follow-through counting; femto-lean/state/ledger.jsonl holds 81 fear_day mentions as live evaluation stamps, not study output.
- THE RESEARCH LINE'S DEFINITION AND NUMBERS EXIST ONLY AS INDEX SUMMARIES, NOT AS FILES ON THIS DISK. The cross-check record (Claude's 2026-10-27-studies, repo Femto-lean branch factory-p3) defines a fear day there as SPY down at least 1% (engine/EVIDENCE.md claim T1: "Fear days (SPY <= -1%)"), with calibrated composite thresholds fear_ret_q10 = -1.11% and fear_spike_q80 = 1.7828 and fear days at 22.87% of discovery days. Follow-through there is the 20-session forward return (xfwd20, net of same-day mean), with 60 sessions as the secondary horizon for the F3 survivor. Denominators visible in the summaries: 467 fear days in 2016-22 (reset2 RESULTS), 111 fear entry days in the holdout (composite RESULTS2). F3 (pullback depth vs the stock's own norm, uptrend names, fear days): IC +0.026 (2016-22) / +0.031 (2023+) at 20 days, t 4.3/4.3; 60-day 2023+ IC +0.062, t 9.8. But the source files themselves are NOT on this disk: the factory-p3 branch is absent from the local femto-lean clone (git rev-parse fails, no docs/research directory in the checkout), and the only local trace is truncated summaries in rea-master-index/entries/batch_160.jsonl and MASTER-INDEX.jsonl. No dated fear-day list, no per-day readings, no per-name follow-through table exists locally to publish or to recompute from.
- RECORDS ON FILE THAT COULD FEED IT ARE THIN AND CALM. The only local VIX history is opt/rea-teardowns/azrael-1587/source/yahoo-vix-daily.json: 23 daily closes, 2026-09-21 to 2026-10-21, maximum 19.99 (never at or above 20, never at or above 25). Its paired price file alpaca-spy-qqq-bars.json holds only 5 SPY/QQQ daily sessions (2026-10-15 to 2026-10-21, 4 computable day returns). Applying the desk's coded fear-day rule to that pair: exactly 1 qualifying day, 2026-10-16, qualifying on the VIX-spike arm only (VIX +21.4% on the day to 19.99; SPY -1.11% that day, above the -1.5% arm; QQQ -2.50%). morgue/data_cache/board_bt_bars.json holds 399 daily sessions for 1,833 names ending 2026-10-23 but carries no VIX series, so the VIX arms cannot be evaluated over it from owned morgue data. Under the research line's SPY <= -1% rule the same 5-session price file also yields exactly that 1 day (2026-10-16).
- BREAKDOWNS: the engine carries none for fear (no by-era split, no by-horizon table, no quintile spread, no per-name list) because the study does not exist in it. The design asks for a names list on fear days plus the cross-era record; the research summaries carry era splits (2016-22 vs 2023+) and 20/60-session horizons, but only as summary prose in an index, not as data. Any breakdown beyond the single qualifying day above would have to be built new from raw history that is not on file (no long VIX history, no long SPY/QQQ daily history in the terminal's owned records beyond the board backtest cache).
- HONEST PAGE SHAPE THAT FOLLOWS: the unpublished state. No fear-day study has ever run in the studies engine and no fear-day list exists on record, so the tool ships the honest unpublished state only: the fear-day definition in one plain sentence (the desk's coded rule), the history actually on file (23 volatility readings, 2026-09-21 to 2026-10-21; 5 index sessions), and the one qualifying day the owned records contain (2026-10-16, with its reading) stated as a count, never dressed as a list with outcomes. No follow-through percentages: with 1 qualifying event the thin-cell rule (fewer than 30 events: counts only) bars every percentage, and no follow-through was ever counted by any engine on disk anyway. Do NOT import the research line's IC figures as this tool's own results: they belong to another desk's study whose files are not on this disk and whose definition (SPY <= -1%) differs from the desk's coded rule.

## 2026-10-28 — Tool 20 State Analogue: ground truth (established in build phase from the files)

- Design (LINEUP-DESIGN-SLATE-TWO tool 22): for any ticker, how much of today's and recent volume was short, day by day; built from the Morgue's FINRA short-volume lane, claimed "built at the lane level; the face is new" and "already landed and collecting". The collecting claim is FALSE on disk today.
- ZERO DAYS STORED, STILL TRUE: the lane (morgue/lanes/finra_shortvol.py, ported 2026-10-27) stores one gzipped file per day at <root>/state/morgue/shortvol/YYYY-MM-DD.json.gz with root = the morgue itself (ext_card.build root=HERE). That directory does not exist: morgue/state/morgue/ holds only ext.json and ledger.jsonl. finra_shortvol.days(root) = []. So tickers on record: none; days on record: 0; freshest day: none. This matches and confirms the Squeeze Watch inspect record (BUILD-PROGRESS Tool 15: "0 days stored"); it has NOT changed since.
- Triple-stamped today: morgue/state/morgue/ext.json lanes section (built 2026-10-28T10:17:55-04:00, for_session 2026-10-28) carries short_volume status no_data, reason "no FINRA day stored yet"; morgue/output/morgue-feed.json (generated 2026-10-28T12:52:03Z) carries the identical lane stamp as_of 2026-10-28. flow_card.lanes_section emits exactly that stamp when days() is empty.
- WHY ZERO: nothing in production ever calls the lane's backfill(). grep over morgue/*.py: backfill appears only in lanes/finra_shortvol.py itself and in tests (tmp_path fixtures only). daily_cycle.py (preopen/postclose steps: board/map/tape/scorecards/studies/publish) has no short-volume step. The lane code is built and fixture-tested; the collection was never wired or run.
- PER-DAY FIELDS THE LANE WOULD STORE (from code + tests/fixtures/fnqc_excerpt.txt): source is FINRA's FNQC daily file (cdn.finra.org/equity/regsho/daily/FNQCshvol{yyyymmdd}.txt, pipe format Date|Symbol|ShortVolume|ShortExemptVolume|TotalVolume|Market, parsed by map_dix.parse_finra). Stored per day: {"date": day, "names": {symbol: [short_volume, total_volume]}} rounded to 0.1; every symbol in that day's file (whole file, not a watchlist). ShortExemptVolume and Market are dropped at parse; the short share (short/total) is derived, never stored. Ticker spelling normalized (BRK/B read as BRK.B). history() returns (date, short, total) strictly before a given date (a day's file posts after that day's close, read for later sessions only); a stored day is never rewritten (store_day returns False if present or data empty); retention KEEP_DAYS 800.
- REFUSAL / MISSING-DAY HANDLING (code + tests/test_morgue_lanes.py): HTTP 404 from fetch_day = no file that day, recorded in backfill's "missing" list, walk continues; any other HTTP status (403, 429, ...) raises HttpError, stops the backfill run, and is reported in "error", never stored as a gap or a zero day. Absence is not zero throughout: empty parse stores nothing. backfill defaults want=260 days, max_new=25 files per run, weekday-only trading-day walk (exchange holidays not skipped, named adaptation in lanes/_common.py). short_pressure (the Prospero-style demand measure) additionally requires MIN_DAYS 60 stored days, so even a started collection could not publish that measure for about three months.
- NOT THIS LANE'S DATA (do not substitute): (1) alpaca-swing-desk/desk/context/shortvol/ holds 9 days (2026-10-02, 05, 06, 07, 08, 09, 12, 13, 14) from a DIFFERENT source and system: FINRA Reg SHO CNMS files (CNMSshvol, not FNQC), written by that desk's own finra_shortvol_snapshot.py, full-file csv.gz plus a marker json (2026-10-14: 12,307 symbols). The morgue lane never reads that path or format. (2) squeezemetrics series = index-level dark-pool share, no per-name short volume. (3) Nasdaq short interest (settlement, twice monthly) in output/short_interest_pit.jsonl is a different measure, already used by Squeeze Watch.
- WHAT THE PAGE CAN HONESTLY PUBLISH TODAY: no per-ticker short-volume figure for any ticker on any day. Only the plain not-yet state: the daily short-sale volume collection has not started, 0 days on record, no freshest day, with the source and its after-close delay stated in public words. Any day-by-day ledger, ratio, or ticker lookup would be fabrication until a backfill actually runs and stores days; the first honest publishable state after collection starts is the stored days themselves (date, short volume, total volume, derived share per name), each day missing from FINRA shown as missing, never zero.

## 2026-10-28 — Tool 23 Filings Wire: inspect phase done
- Design (slate two, tool 23): one ticker's SEC filings and reported numbers digested: what was filed, when, and the headline figures. Slate claims "built at the lane level; the digest face is new" — the code is built, but the data store is EMPTY, which the slate does not say.
- Lane on disk (morgue/lanes/sec_facts.py): parses SEC companyfacts (us-gaap, USD and shares units only) into compact per-fact records carrying period end, period start, value, filed date, form type, fiscal year and fiscal period. Concepts extracted (14): revenue, net income (earnings), operating cash flow, long-term debt, assets, liabilities, equity, gross profit, cost of revenue, operating income, current assets, current liabilities, retained earnings, shares. NO cash-balance figure is extracted, and debt is long-term debt only. Derived outputs of fundamentals(): ROE, asset growth, gross profitability, accruals, Piotroski F, Altman Z (needs a market cap input the filings do not carry).
- Coverage on disk today: ZERO names. morgue/state/morgue/facts/ does not exist (no .json.gz files); the published lane status in output/ext_card.json and output/morgue-feed.json (as_of 2026-10-28) is sec_facts no_data, "no company's facts stored yet". Nothing in the daily cycle calls the lane's refresh(); fundamentals() is exercised only by tests on fixture data. So: no figures per name, no period ends, and NO freshest filing date on record for company facts.
- Period rule: annual() publishes annual figures only: flows must span 330 to 400 days; balances are period-end instants (fiscal-year only when no start date). Quarterly figures would be stored in the raw facts but are not read by the annual/fundamentals path.
- Per-period fallback (adopted cure, in the code): concept tags are read in the lane's listed order and the fallback is PER PERIOD: the first tag wins for each period it covers, later tags fill periods the first tag lacks (previously the first tag with any annual figure won for all periods). Per period, the latest filing at or before the read date wins, so a restatement counts only from its own filing date. Same adopted commit cured the compressed-response decoding.
- Filing events: NOT recorded as events anywhere. No filing list, no submissions fetch, no event log exists in this lane; the only filing metadata is the form type and filed date attached to each individual figure inside a stored facts file, of which there are none. (Separate and not fed by this lane: output/inst_bundle.json, as_of 2026-10-26, 50 names, carries insider/ownership/short scores only, no reported figures; its provenance records insider Form 4 latest filing dates for 43 names, freshest 2026-10-26, which are insider filings, not company financial reports.)
- Honest publishable state today: the page can only say plainly that no company's reported figures are on record yet. Publishing any number first requires running the lane's refresh for chosen companies (needs the operator SEC identity configured), storing their facts, then digesting the annual figures with each figure's period end, filed date and form type.

## 2026-10-28 — Tool 24 Dark Prints: inspect phase done
- Design (Slate Two tool 24): one ticker's DIX-style dark pressure reading plus recent large off-exchange prints; status in the slate is "partial: construction exists; per-ticker coverage floors must be set and proven."
- Index-level ground truth (fresh, proven): morgue/output/map_card.json rebuilt 2026-10-28T12:52:01Z carries session 2026-10-27: SPY constituent-weighted DIX 43.9% (0.4392, graded, 501 of 503 S&P 500 names used, 99.6% coverage, weights = session dollar volume close x volume from Alpaca SIP, declared OURS); per-ETF own ratios SPY 53.4% / QQQ 31.8% / IWM 46.6%, simple average 44.0%. Same figures in morgue-feed.json (as_of_session 2026-10-27). Ledger map rows carry non-null per-ETF DIX for sessions 2026-10-23, 2026-10-26, 2026-10-27 only (3 sessions).
- How it is built (morgue/map_dix.py): the FINRA FNQC daily file (off-exchange short volume / total volume per symbol) is fetched live and parsed IN MEMORY (fetch_full, walk-back up to 7 days to the newest file); constituent_dix dollar-weights the per-name ratios into the single index value. The per-name ratios are NEVER stored: not in map_card.json, not in the feed, not in the ledger (ledger keeps only the 3 ETF ratios plus, on the SPY row, the aggregate dix_index block).
- Per-name readings on record: NONE. The stored per-name history lane (morgue/lanes/finra_shortvol.py: one gz file per day under state/morgue/shortvol/, each holding every name's short and total) exists as code plus tests, but has never collected: state/morgue/ contains only ext.json and ledger.jsonl, no shortvol directory; the feed and ext.json both stamp short_volume "no_data: no FINRA day stored yet"; Tool 22's published data.json independently confirms 0 days on record, no stock on record, freshest day null. Per-name coverage today = 0 names, 0 sessions. No backfill call exists in daily_cycle.py.
- What the per-name figure WOULD mean (from the code, not from stored data): for one name on one session, off-exchange (dark/ATS) short-sale volume divided by that name's total volume that day, from the FINRA daily file, which posts after the close. It is a short-sale share of off-exchange volume, not a buy/sell pressure gauge and not a count of individual prints. Completeness per name can only be stated per stored day (name present with total > 0, or missing, never zero); with 0 stored days no per-name completeness statement exists.
- Derivable but not on record: a build COULD read one session's per-name ratios off a fresh live fetch of the newest FNQC file (the same in-memory parse the Map already does, e.g. the 501 of 503 names that had data for 2026-10-27), but that yields exactly one session, no history, no stored evidence, and disappears when the run ends. Any multi-session per-name page requires the shortvol lane to actually start collecting (or a one-off backfill run of its existing backfill() function, want=260, max_new=25 per run).
- Large off-exchange prints per name: DO NOT EXIST anywhere on record. The Flow Tape (morgue/tape.py, tape_card.py, output/tape_card.json generated 2026-10-27T20:46:23Z) is OPTIONS prints for SPY/QQQ/IWM only (defaults in tape_card.py; 476,456 / 366,373 / 147,726 prints, 25 alerts each, session 2026-10-27); it holds no equity prints and no other names. The FNQC file itself is daily per-name aggregates, not individual prints, so no large-print list can be read off it. The Board's darkPool leaf is explicitly None / no-data ("no free route", board_features.py, inst_bundle.py).
- Honest publishable page today: an index-level Dark Prints page only: the SPY constituent dark reading for 2026-10-27 (43.9%, 501 of 503 names, with its coverage stated), the three index funds' own off-exchange short shares for the same session, and at most the 3 ledger sessions of history (2026-10-23, 2026-10-26, 2026-10-27). A per-ticker page CANNOT honestly publish any name's reading or any large off-exchange prints; its per-name section must render the plain not-collecting / not-available state (the Tool 22 pattern), or the build must first store at least one FNQC day and state coverage as that one session and the names present in it.
- ADDENDUM (same inspect pass, found by a disk-wide sweep after the entries above): a LEGACY per-name store exists OUTSIDE the morgue, at alpaca-swing-desk/desk/context/shortvol/ (the old swing desk, not the morgue/terminal lineage, not read by any lineup tool). It holds 9 stored sessions, 2026-10-02 to 2026-10-14, each the full FINRA CNMS daily file (12,236 to 12,465 symbols per day; freshest 2026-10-14 with 12,307 symbols, every row carrying total volume > 0) with a computed short_ratio = ShortVolume / TotalVolume per name (example: AAPL 0.5748 on 2026-10-14). Three limits: (1) it is the CNMS file (all reporting facilities), NOT the FNQC off-exchange facility file the DIX construction is defined on, so its per-name ratio is a whole-market short share, not the DIX dark-pool ratio, and the two must never be substituted; (2) it stopped collecting after 2026-10-14, 9 sessions behind the freshest Map session (2026-10-27), so anything read from it is stale on its face; (3) no morgue or lineup machinery reads it (Tool 22's refresh reads only the morgue state and correctly reports 0 days). It is a possible one-off historical source a build could explicitly adopt and label, never a silent substitute for the missing FNQC per-name record. The headline verdict is unchanged: per-name DIX-style dark readings on the morgue record = 0 names, 0 sessions; large off-exchange prints per name = none anywhere, including this store (daily aggregates only).

## 2026-10-28 — Tool 33 Regime Dial: inspect phase done (no files changed besides this entry)
- Slate (LINEUP-DESIGN-SLATE-TWO, tool 33): one plain sentence about what kind of market this is, backed by a handful of checks each shown; describes the regime, never forecasts; check set must be named, frozen, graded.
- Candidate checks from published data.json files, freshest figures: (1) Breadth long trend, site/breadth-meter: 46.9% above 200-session average (846 of 1,805), session 2026-10-23. (2) Breadth short trend: 44.7% above 50-session average (813 of 1,817), 2026-10-23. (3) Day direction: 34.4% rose (625 rose, 1,177 fell, 15 unchanged of 1,817), 2026-10-23. (4) Sector leadership, site/sector-rotation, to 2026-10-23: leaders defensive (Utilities +6.73%, Consumer Staples +6.65%, Health Care +4.83% over 21 sessions), Materials lagging -2.54%, Information Technology turning (-1.99% 21s / +2.10% 5s). (5) Position vs flip, site/morning-map, session 2026-10-27: all three indexes below their flip (SPY 771.65 vs 773.37, 0.22% below; QQQ 723.47 vs 727.83, 0.60% below; IWM 280.20 vs 288.45, 2.94% below), positioning amplifying on all three. (6) Dark reading, morning-map 2026-10-27: SPY dark-pool fund reading 53.4, SPY combined constituent reading 43.9 (constituent reading exists for SPY only); QQQ fund 31.8, IWM fund 46.6. (7) Fear gauge, site/expected-move: freshest on record 19.03 (tied to the 2026-10-22 close, map edition 2026-10-23); QQQ edition 16.68 at 2026-10-21. (8) Wall behaviour, site/wall-check graded through 2026-10-27: only 6 graded tests, under that tool's own thin floor of 30 (flip respected 6 of 6, lower wall held 6 of 6, upper wall broke 2 of 2 reached, both IWM) — too thin to be a dial check; supporting line only until the graded count reaches 30.
- Sessions mismatch: breadth and rotation end Friday 2026-10-23; the fear gauge record ends 2026-10-22/23; the morning map and wall grading are Tuesday 2026-10-27. Breadth is two sessions behind the map. Honest handling proposed: every check printed with its own session date beside its figure; the sentence labelled as resting on checks from those named dates, never blended into one "today"; a fixed staleness rule (a check more than 2 sessions behind the freshest check is shown but does not count; if fewer than 4 of the 6 checks count, the dial reads no clear reading) stated in the register triple before building.
- Proposed fixed plain rule (thresholds are round numbers and the fear levels 20/25 are the Fear Day List's own published thresholds, not tuned to today): checks = long trend (share above 200-session average vs half), short trend (share above 50-session average vs half), day direction (rose vs fell), leadership (top three sectors over 21 sessions growth-led vs defensive-led), position (SPY above or below its flip), fear (gauge under 20 calm, 20 to 25 uneasy, 25 or more stressed). Kinds: Stressed = fear at 25 or more, whatever the rest say. Trending up = both trend checks above half and SPY above the flip. Trending down = both trend checks below half and SPY below the flip. Range-bound = fear under 20 and neither trending condition met and the day check not strongly one-sided. Mixed / no clear reading = anything else, any disagreement the kinds above do not cover, or too few fresh checks. Rule prints on the page in plain words before its result; the sentence is labelled as the dial's reading of the checks.
- Nothing else changed in this phase; the build phase registers the triple in the refresh script before building.

  1. alpaca-swing-desk/desk/research/bilbo-options/iv_snapshots.jsonl (read as data only; iv_snapshot.py never imported or run): 1,198 rows = 20 tickers (AAPL, AMD, AMZN, AVGO, BAC, COIN, DIS, GOOGL, HOOD, INTC, JPM, META, MSFT, MU, NFLX, NVDA, PLTR, SMCI, TSLA, UBER) x 12 dated readings each, 2026-10-02 to 2026-10-14 (calendar-daily runs incl. Sat 10-03, Sun 10-04, Sat 10-10; no 10-11). Each dated reading = 4 to 5 strike rows (INTC has 4 on 10-12 and 10-14; all others 5) at ONE expiry_dte only (21 to 27 days across the run, rolling; BAC's freshest reading is 37). Each row carries date, symbol, strike, that strike's iv, underlying price, target_strike, atr14; no null IVs; zero duplicate (date,symbol,strike,dte) keys in the file as it stands. A single IV per reading requires a stated reduction (e.g. nearest-to-underlying strike); the rows are per-strike, NOT per-expiry: no term structure is in this ledger.
  2. alpaca-swing-desk/desk/context/sentiment/options/iv_skew/<date>.json, 8 dated files 2026-10-05 to 2026-10-14 (weekdays 05,06,07,08,09,12,13,14): 41 symbol keys, 40 with readings (SKYQ skipped every day, no option contracts), 8 dated readings each except AEHR, CORZ, CVNA, HIVE, LASR, TEM, VELO with 7. Each reading carries ONE blended iv30 number (30-day target) plus skew25, expected_move_30d, underlying, n_contracts, and the 1 or 2 expiration dates it was blended from, but NO per-expiry IV values: no term structure here either. Its own iv30_pctile_60d field is null until >=5 history days by its own note and is non-null only on the last 3 files, computed off 5 to 7 prior readings.
  3. alpaca-swing-desk/desk/hidden_state/iron_condor_iv_history.jsonl: 26 rows = 13 small-cap symbols (AEHR, ARM, ARQQ, AXTI, CORZ, DGXX, HIVE, IREN, NBIS, POET, SNDK, VELO, WYFI) x exactly 2 dated readings (2026-10-13, 2026-10-14), each a single bare iv number with date and symbol only (no strike, expiry or underlying). iron_condor_iv_baseline.json confirms rows 26, min 2026-10-13, max 2026-10-14.
- Freshest IV reading on record anywhere: 2026-10-14 (all three ledgers end there), 14 days old today (2026-10-28). Any page must date-stamp readings and show that age; nothing here is current.
- Percentile honesty (thin-cell rule): a percentile is arithmetically computable for the bilbo tickers (12 readings each) and the skew tickers (7 to 8 each), and not meaningfully for the iron-condor names (2 each). But 12 readings is one reading per ~8 percentile points, and every ticker in every ledger sits far below the floors this lineup already uses elsewhere (30 graded tests on Wall Check, 126 sessions on State Analogue). Verdict for the build: NO ticker honestly supports a published history percentile today; publish the current reading, its date, the reading count, and the history's low-to-high range (counts/facts), percentile counts-only until a stated floor (recommend 30 dated readings) is reached. Term structure: publish NONE, no source carries per-expiry IVs for the same date; the slate's term-structure element waits for the chain archive.
- What the page can honestly publish today: per-ticker latest IV readings dated 2026-10-14 with counts and ranges from the bilbo 20 (richest series, 12 each) and/or the skew 40 (single iv30 each, 7 to 8), labelled stale (last recorded October 14, 2026), no percentiles, no term structure, no SPY/QQQ/IWM. Nothing else changed in this phase.

## Skew Lens (tool 27) - INSPECT 2026-10-28
- SCOPE: ground truth only, nothing built or changed. Sources read as data only (iv_snapshot.py never imported or run): ledger 1 = alpaca-swing-desk/desk/research/bilbo-options/iv_snapshots.jsonl; ledger 2 = desk/context/sentiment/options/iv_skew/<date>.json (8 files); ledger 3 = single readings (no per-strike content, not skew material). Plus the chain archive state (morgue ext.json chain_archive stamp + site/chain-explorer/data.json) and the tape record (site/flow-tape/data.json).
- Ledger 1 shape (verified by full recompute): 1,198 rows, 240 (date,name) groups = 20 names x 12 dates 2026-10-02 to 2026-10-14, 5 strike rows per group (4 for INTC on 10-12 and 10-14), ONE expiry per group (expiry_dte only, 21 to 37 days; no calendar expiry date). Row fields: date, symbol, expiry_dte, strike, iv, underlying, target_strike, atr14. NO put/call side field anywhere in the file.
- Ledger 1 strike geometry kills the example construction: strikes cluster around target_strike, which sits 1.1% to 5.0% ABOVE the recorded price. Strike/price ratios span only 0.9535 to 1.1189 across all 240 groups; the deepest below-price strike in the whole ledger is 4.65% under (BAC). 83 of 240 groups have NO strike below the price at all; on the freshest day (2026-10-14) 10 of 20 names have every strike above the price. There is no strike anywhere near 10% below the price, so a put-at-minus-10% minus call-at-plus-10% figure (or any matched-distance pair at +/-10%) cannot be computed for ANY name on ANY day. Even a nearer matched pair fails the adopted standard: the rows carry no side, so no row is on record as a put reading or a call reading; inferring side from strike position would be an estimate, which the standard forbids.
- Ledger 2: 41 names x 8 dated files (2026-10-05 to 2026-10-14), each reading carries a precomputed skew25 number (33 to 40 of 41 names per day; SKYQ missing every day; freshest day 40 of 41). BUT the files carry no per-strike records at all (one blended iv30 + skew25 + expected move per name, target 30 days, blended from 1 to 2 expirations) and no statement anywhere in the data of how skew25 was constructed. It cannot be recomputed or verified from per-strike records, so it fails the adopted standard for a published skew figure. Only overlap with ledger 1 is MU. No SPY/QQQ/IWM.
- Index names: SPY/QQQ/IWM have NO per-strike IV records in any source. Chain archive is empty (ext.json chain_archive stamp "no_data / no chain archived yet" as of 2026-10-28; chain-explorer publishes 0 snapshots for SPY, QQQ, IWM). Tape records (flow-tape, session 2026-10-27, SPY/QQQ/IWM) carry contract kind, strike, expiry, contracts and premium but NO implied volatility field anywhere, so the tape cannot yield a skew figure. Ledger 1 and 2 contain no index names.
- VERDICT: publishable skew figures under the adopted standard = ZERO names, ZERO days. What CAN be published today is only the honest empty state: no name on record carries put-side and call-side per-strike readings at matched distances from the same day's price at the same expiry, the per-strike ledger that exists is a one-sided ladder of strikes at and above the price for 20 large names over 12 days ending 2026-10-14, and the index names have no chain on record yet. The Lens's real figure waits for the chain archive (which would carry both sides per strike) to accumulate snapshots. Any build must register this construction exactly and show no figure, never an estimate, until a name/day/expiry carries both sides.

## 2026-10-28 — Tool 29 Greeks Sketch: inspect phase done (no files changed besides this entry)
- SCOPE: Surface=ground truth only for site/greeks-sketch/ (not yet built); Constraints=Payoff Sketch conventions, adopted oracle only, rate/dividend as visible reader inputs, no data.json, four measures only; Outcome=inputs/outputs/conventions/test grid below; Proof=files read this phase; Edges=slate says multi-leg position, workflow build spec narrows to ONE contract (stock price, strike, days, vol, rate, dividend, call/put) — build follows the narrower single-contract spec.
- CORRECTION to brief: there is no oracle/bs.py. Oracle dir holds codex_app.js + crosscheck_payoff.py + PROVENANCE.md + crosscheck_results.json. European engine = blackScholesEuropean (codex_app.js line 2093); our engine it was proven against is ~/workspace/morgue/bs.py (crosscheck_payoff.py imports it; tolerance 1e-4 relative).
- Oracle blackScholesEuropean(spot, strike, daysToExpiration, ivPct, riskFreeRatePct, dividendYieldPct, type): years=days/365, sigma=ivPct/100, continuous dividend yield. Returns per share: theoreticalPrice ($), delta (per $1 stock move; call e^-qT*N(d1), put e^-qT*(N(d1)-1)), gamma (per $1; same call/put; e^-qT*phi(d1)/(S*sigma*sqrtT) — NOTE morgue bs.gamma and the Payoff page omit the e^-qT factor, identical only at q=0, so q>0 cases are mandatory in the grid), vegaPerPctPoint ($ per 1 percentage point of vol = raw vega/100), thetaPerDay ($ per calendar day = thetaAnnual/365), rhoPerPctPoint ($ per 1 point of rate). Rho is computed by the oracle but NOT shown (build spec: four measures). Vanna/charm exist only in morgue bs.py, not in the oracle European function, NOT shown. American CRR (crrAmericanOption/americanCrrGreeks, same theta-per-day and vega-per-point conventions) is in the adopted set but NOT used: this page is European only, like Payoff Sketch.
- INPUTS (all visible, plain labels, live update on input): stock price $ (default 100), strike price $ (100), call or put (call), days until expiry (30), volatility % per year (25), interest rate % per year (4.5, the Payoff Sketch value now exposed not hidden), dividend yield % per year (0). No premium, no contracts count, no legs.
- OUTPUTS (per share, for one option on one share): model price $, plus four cards in Payoff Sketch style (name, signed value, one-line plain meaning): Delta = $ price change if stock moves $1; Gamma = change in delta if stock moves $1; Theta = $ change as one day passes; Vega = $ change if volatility moves 1 point.
- CONVENTIONS PAGE MUST PRINT (plain words, no model/file names, no disclaimer copy): European-style, settled only on expiry day, no early exercise; year = 365 calendar days; theta per day, vega per 1 point; rate and dividend are the yearly percents the reader set above; everything worked out in the browser, nothing typed is sent anywhere (Payoff Sketch wording); free line in header. Edge rule from Payoff page code (oracle European function has no guard and divides by sqrtT/sigma): at days=0 or vol=0 price is intrinsic value and greeks show 0 — verify tests this as a page rule, not against the oracle formula.
- VERIFY TEST GRID (page JS vs blackScholesEuropean extracted from oracle/codex_app.js, tolerance 1e-4 relative, near-zero judged on absolute error, comparing price, delta, gamma, thetaPerDay, vegaPerPctPoint): (A) core factorial r=4.5 q=0: S in {50,80,100,120,150} x K in {80,100,120} x days in {7,30,90,365} x vol% in {15,25,40,80} x {call,put} = 480 cases. (B) rate/dividend: S in {80,100,120} x K=100 x days in {30,365} x vol% in {20,35} x r% in {0,4.5,5} x q% in {0,2} x {call,put} = 144 cases (catches the gamma e^-qT factor and rho-free price/delta/theta dependence). (C) anchors: Hull S100 K100 365d vol20 r5 q0 call 10.450576 / put 5.573518; gamma blocker S100 K80 30d vol20 r4.5 q0, and at r0 vs 3.1993e-5; put-call parity on crosscheck cases (100,100,180d,35,4.5,2), (120,90,90d,30,3,1), (80,110,30d,45,5,0). (D) page edges: days=0 and vol=0 give intrinsic price and zero greeks.

- Ground truth read directly: slate-two tool 36 (personal bench, daily check-in, "anything happening in my names?") and site/second-opinion/data.json (md5 a714b7c300791e7d60824858f1e87305, state published, session 2026-10-27). - Record shape: 1,838 unique uppercase symbols; per name exactly {symbol, families_speaking, directional[], price|null, prints|null, positioning|null, filings|null} plus price_silent_reason on 5 names. Six families are listed at top level; Company facts and News and events are silent for every name (reasons in silent_reasons), so the effective ceiling is 3 speaking (only SPY/QQQ/IWM: price+prints+positioning, all cautious). Census: 1,792 names 1 family (price only), 38 names 2 (price+filings), 3 names 3, 5 names 0 (ALTM, FREY, GOEV, SMAR, SQ: too little price history).
- A pulse line can show, from this record alone: symbol; families speaking as "N of 6 families"; each directional family's direction in the record's own plain words (positive / mixed / cautious — price stance on 1,833 names; prints and positioning stances on the 3 index funds only); agreement at a glance derivable from directional[] (one direction only for 1,792+38 names, since filings carries no direction; all-agree only for the 3 index funds). Price facts available per name: close, score of 100, confirming checks x of 8, passed-every-check flag (43 positive = qualified with score>=60 pattern per the Second Opinion's own reading rule). Filings facts where present (38 names): named readings of 100, insider transaction count + latest date, short % + days to cover + settlement date — facts, never a direction. Prints/positioning detail exists only for SPY/QQQ/IWM.
- Freshest session: top-level session 2026-10-27 (Tuesday) is the scored day behind price (and the prints day; positioning dix_date also 2026-10-27). Filings lines are older and dated on their face: filings_as_of 2026-10-26, per-name insider_latest and si_settlement (e.g. 2026-10-15). A pulse line stamps the record's session, never "today".
- List mechanics: add a name (typed symbol, trimmed/uppercased, duplicates ignored), remove per line, list persisted in localStorage on the reader's machine only and restored on revisit; no account, no send. No localStorage precedent exists anywhere in site/ yet — this tool introduces it. A name not among the 1,838 on record gets a plain not-on-record line and stays on the reader's list; no data is invented for it.
- Distinctness: the Second Opinion page is a one-name deep read (picker over all 1,838, all six families expanded with tables, silent reasons and receipt). Watchlist Pulse is the reader's own subset on one screen, one compact strip per name (families speaking, directions in plain words, freshest session), read at a glance; no per-family deep tables, no categories table (present for only 50 names anyway).
- Nothing changed in this phase except this progress entry; no site files touched.

- History measured directly from the file (not from docs): 1,833 names; rows are [date, open, high, low, close, volume], close at index 4 (the same index refresh_breadth.py reads); union calendar is exactly 399 sessions, 2025-03-25 to 2026-10-23; zero names have interior gaps (every series is contiguous from its first session to its last). 1,792 names hold all 399 sessions. 41 names are short (late listings): shortest ADIG 58, MBGL 81, MFP 83, HONA 92, FDXF 102, MAG 112, WDRN 113, AVEX 132, DRAM 142, VGNT 143; 4 names under 100 sessions, 13 under 200, 41 under 399. 16 names do not trade on the freshest session (2026-10-23): 3 stopped within days (ACA, QRVO, BLFS/PSKY/WBD in early October) and the rest weeks to months earlier (MAG last traded 2025-09-03; CYBR 2026-02-10; LITM 2026-03-13; BITF 2026-04-02). Index funds SPY, QQQ, IWM, DIA and the sector funds (XLF, XLK, XLE spot-checked) are all in the history, so a plain-words reference set needs no outside data.
- Register triple for the build (stated here, to be restated at the top of the refresh script before building): construction is Pearson correlation of daily returns (each day's close divided by the name's own previous traded close, minus one) over the most recent 126 sessions of the history (sessions 2026-04-27 to 2026-10-23 on the current history); a pair is published only when the two names share at least 40 overlapping return days inside that window, and the overlap count is printed beside every pair; the correlation number itself is printed beside its plain-words label. Window and floor sized from the data: in the last 126 sessions, 1,826 of 1,833 names have at least 40 sessions and 1,811 have full 126 coverage; the 7 names under the floor (APLS 13, CTRA 8, CWEN.A 4, BITF 0, CYBR 0, LITM 0, MAG 0 in-window sessions) stopped trading too long ago and are shown as not enough shared history, never given invented partners.
- Design (the honest shape for a static page): all pairs of 1,833 names is 1,679,028 pairs and cannot ship. The page ships the daily returns themselves for the bounded 126-session window for all 1,833 names: measured 1.26 MB as compact JSON (0.42 MB gzipped), inside the site's existing precedent (watchlist-pulse data.json is 581 KB). The reader picks up to 10 names; the page computes the at most 45 pairs among the picks in the browser from those returns, pair by pair on shared days only. On top of that, the refresh precomputes each name's closest twins across the whole list (refresh-side all-pairs over full-coverage names measured at about 1.3 seconds; median strongest twin |r| 0.71), so picking one name also shows its closest matches anywhere, not only among the picks. Rejected alternative: precomputing each name only against a fixed reference set cannot answer the tool's own question (are two of MY names the same trade?) for two non-reference picks, so it is not the design.
- Plain-words scale the page will print (correlation printed beside the words): 0.70 to 1.00 moves together closely; 0.40 to 0.69 moves together somewhat; 0.15 to 0.39 barely moves together; -0.14 to 0.14 no clear link; -0.39 to -0.15 tends to move opposite, a little; -0.69 to -0.40 tends to move opposite; -1.00 to -0.70 moves opposite closely. (Bands stated here for the build to freeze verbatim in the refresh's definitions, in plain words, no internal names, no file names, no disclaimer copy.)
- Open items for the build phase: pick-list mechanics mirror Watchlist Pulse (typed symbol, uppercase, duplicates ignored, list kept in the reader's browser only); names outside the 1,833 get a plain not-on-record line; pairs under the 40-day floor print the shared-day count and no label; the twin list per name must carry each twin's overlap count the same way.

- Ground truth, from the files read: SIP-grade share volume DOES exist on record, so the tool publishes real figures, not the honest empty state. The daily price history behind the scoring run (morgue/data_cache/board_bt_bars.json: 1,833 names, 399 sessions, March 25, 2025 to October 23, 2026) carries a volume on every bar (no missing, negative, or out-of-order volumes). Its loader (board_backtest.py load_bars) writes it only through board_card.py fetch_bars, which declares daily bars from the consolidated tape feed (feed=sip, split-adjusted, completed sessions only). The morgue tape records are options prints, not share volume, and were not used; no Yahoo volume of any kind was used.
- Construction (stated in the register triple and printed on the page): measured session is the freshest history session, Friday, October 23, 2026; a name's usual level is the average of its share volume over its own previous 20 trading days (calendar September 25 to October 22, 2026 for a name that traded every day), rounded to the nearest whole share; the multiple is the measured volume divided by that rounded usual level, shown to two decimals; the floor is an unrounded ratio of at least 2.00 (a rounding cure during build: ENVA at 1.998 and KRC at 1.997 do NOT pass merely because their printed multiple would round to 2.00). Thin-cell rule: no percentage anywhere on the page; counts print with denominators and each name's figures are its own measurements.
- Real figures carried: 1,817 of the 1,833 scored names traded on the measured session and were measured; 16 did not trade that day and show nothing; 61 names reached at least twice their usual level, ranked by the multiple: top LKQ 7.18x (23,405,884 shares against a usual 3,258,571), then CART 5.08x, SLM 4.59x, CORT 4.20x, FLG and FCFS 3.92x. An independent recompute from the source bars reproduced data.json's list exactly (all 61 names, volumes, usual levels, multiples, order).
- Refresh fails closed: a provenance gate re-checks that the fetch behind the history still declares the consolidated tape feed for daily bars and that the loader still routes through it (any volume whose provenance is not SIP stops the refresh); the scored run's card summary vs its full list (checked/scored/too little data/passed, session) must agree; the history's name set must equal the scored set exactly; any missing, non-numeric, or negative volume, repeated or out-of-order session, a measured name with fewer than 20 earlier bars, or a zero usual level stops it. Tamper proofs: a scratch copy with the fetch's feed declaration changed exits 1 naming the provenance failure and publishes nothing; a scratch copy with the card's scored count changed to 1,800 exits 1 naming the mismatch.
- Refresh is idempotent: two runs byte-identical (md5 bd93899eb0f283b612c93c460a2e79c1); all dates come from the bars themselves, never the clock.
- Proof: Node DOM-shim render against the real data.json passed (stale banner shown because the measured session 2026-10-23 is older than the last completed session; summary "61 names"; 62 table rows including the header; LKQ's volume, usual level and 7.18x all rendered; construction, baseline window, and floor printed; receipt with coverage and the 399-session history span). - Replica found and read: ~/workspace/opt/rea-teardowns/thestrat/replica/strat_replica.py (+ README.md); its own suite re-run in this phase: 21 tests, OK. - Per-bar labels from classify_bars (vendor terms): scenario 1 (inside: neither prior side taken), 2u (prior high only), 2d (prior low only), 3 (both sides; plain "3" from daily OHLC only, 3u/3d needs first-side data daily bars do not carry); color green/red/flat; shape hammer/shooter/None (whole body in top/bottom third of range, boundary counts; zero-range bar no shape); failed_2 flag exactly "2u red: failed 2u, potential 3" or "2d green: failed 2d, potential 3" (colour only), else None. First bar of a series: scenario None.
- Inputs per name: OHLC per bar (t,o,h,l,c; volume ignored except passthrough). Minimum 2 consecutive daily bars for today's + prior scenario labels; 3 bars for the multi-bar patterns (find_signals/completed_sequences look back to i-2). No other input; in practice the full history is fed and the last bars read.
- Combination patterns the replica itself defines: find_signals setup patterns ending at a bar: 2-2 reversal, hammer, shooter, momo hammer, momo shooter, 3-2, 3-2-2, 2-1-2, 3-1-2, I-O-I, 1-3 rev strat, 1-2-2 rev strat, 1-3-2 (each a Signal: pattern, sequence string e.g. "2d-2u", direction, kind, trigger/target/level_of_reclaim/failure_level, setup_end_index, status; for the freshest bar there is no next bar so status is "setup" for every signal). completed_sequences labels: "<2x>-<2y> 2-2 reversal", "<a>-<b>-<c> 2-2-2 shotgun", "2-1-2 completed 2u/2d", "3-1-2 completed 2u/2d". Standalone detectors also in the file but not per-name scan output: compound_3, pivot_machine_gun, kicker, engulfing, is_failed_2_reclaim; FTFC/sim-breaks need multi-timeframe opens / a basket, not daily bars per name.

- One cell = one (forecaster, target, context) triple. Forecaster: a single registered rule or score per name per day (market lane: per session on SPY). Target carries the horizon: up_excess_5/20/60 = forward return (close[i+h]/open[i+1]-1) minus the same-day same-state-group mean, rank-IC scored; realized_vol_20 = log realized volatility over the next 20 sessions; spy_rv_5 = SPY realized vol over the next 5 sessions. Context: "all" pooled, one dimension value, or a pair (never triples; sector missing, no cell) over 6 dims defined in desk/forecast/contexts.py: fear (fear/calm), spy200 (above_rising/above_falling/below), vix (lo/mid/hi trailing tercile), tier (q1-q5 daily dollar-volume quintile), age (new under 252 sessions/old), state (UP/RESET). 135 context cells per cross-sectional forecaster x target, 30 per market pair (run 1 report).
- YES, the 63 grade-A cells ARE individually listable from the record itself: rerun report section 6 lists all 63 rows (counted 63; set-equality verified against the record's own replicate.compare run over the two JSONs: identical 63 keys). Composition: vol.garch/realized_vol_20 48, tai.pct/realized_vol_20 7, vol.gex_pct/spy_rv_5 2, avoid.pump/up_excess_20 2, veto.gapup10/up_excess_5 2 + up_excess_20 1, avoid.deadcat/up_excess_20 1. That is 57 volatility + 6 stock-direction (all avoid flags, no buy-side cell); 7 forecaster x target pairs, 6 forecasters. Many are overlapping context slices of one pair, not 63 distinct strategies.
- Per-cell fields: section 6 carries forecaster, target, cell (coded dim=value string), sign, rerun discovery mean, rerun holdout signed mean (signed block t), net per opportunity (dash for vol cells), run 1 holdout signed mean (t). The two JSONs carry one row per cell (all cells, 25 columns): forecaster, target, cell, status, verdict, disc_dates, disc_obs, disc_mean, disc_t, disc_t_nw, disc_p, q, bh, sign, wf_folds, wf_agree, wf_t, hold_dates, hold_obs, hold_signed, hold_t_signed, execution_net, net_t, n_opportunities, capacity_usd. Grade A itself is NOT a JSON field: it is the join in desk/forecast/replicate.py (run 1 promoted-B row + rerun tested, same sign, rerun verdict promoted-B); section 6 is its materialized list and REGISTRY.md carries it forward at pair level only.
- Recipe readability caveat: the reports give the recipe in coded form only. Plain definitions live on the same branch, scattered: contexts.py (every dimension value), targets.py (targets), adapter headers (avoid.py: deadcat = close/60-bar high <= 0.6 and 5-day return >= +15%; pump = 5-day return >= +30% on volume >= 3x the prior 60-bar median; survivors.py: gapup10 = open/prior close - 1 >= +10%; vendors.py: vol.garch = fixed GARCH(1,1) log-vol, tai.pct = ln(close x volume) percentile in its trailing 252; regime.py: vol.gex_pct = GEX percentile in trailing 252, sign -1 on realized vol). REGISTRY.md gives source pointer, targets and sign per forecaster but does not repeat the P1 avoid set. A readable card therefore requires joining those sources; nothing may be invented.
- Full tally, run 1 (DEEP, 6,385 names, 2016-01-04 to 2026-10-19, split 2023-01-01, BH q 0.10, min 30 dates/300 obs): 15,135 cells, 11,150 tested, 3,985 untested, 2,986 BH-pass. Inputs per name per day (today.snapshot): sym, as_of, bar_closed, owned (True/False/None), context dims (fear, spy200, vix, tier, age, state; unknown = None and never matches), values {fid: {x, pct}} (flag on if x>0.5; score on if pct above 0.8 quintile for sign +1, at or below 0.2 for sign -1), this name's own measured per-side cost, dv20, optional plan.
- A name gets a non-abstain label only when ALL of: a promoted-book cell of grade A and status promoted matches every dim it names AND its forecaster is on today; gates pass (bar is a closed session, cost measured, dv20 measured/in domain); the cell has at least 30 dates in every window it was scored on; and the lower bound (Newey-West, lags h-1, one-sided 95%, z=1.645) of its per-date utility (gross less sides traded x own cost: 2 buy, 1 sell/trim, 0 avoid/hold) over the best alternative (flat included), taken as the minimum over the cell's windows, is above zero. One name is one bet: the highest-bound passing cell speaks, others listed as supporting, never added; passing cells pointing both ways = conflict = ABSTAIN. Labels: not owned BUY/WAIT/AVOID; owned HOLD/TRIM/SELL (SELL only if the same bound at 99%, z=2.326, is also above zero, else TRIM); ownership unknown = ABSTAIN. WAIT = evidence supports a buy (or an exit) but the plan consistency check or the kill switch holds it back. Volatility cells never set a label; they are listed as risk context only. Probation (grade B) and demoted cells are shown, never routed. Default on any failure: ABSTAIN, reason recorded.

NO long (up) cell reached grade A, so on the recorded book BUY/HOLD cannot fire from evidence; AVOID can, and TRIM/SELL on held names.

## 2026-10-28 — Tool 47 Decay Watch: inspect phase done
- Design (Slate Three, tool 47): watch the Promoted Book age; publish only movements the records themselves carry (a standing recorded at two or more times, or a dated probation/promotion event); decay is a statement about grades, never an opinion; never re-grade here.
- Ground truth: exactly ONE grading of the book exists. The frozen book (promoted-book-1, sha256 56728a593611bee2835f9f7ee4766cee65b08a0b6336c34b6d2947b6241c1092, matching the pin in the second search's report and the factory freeze pin) is a single snapshot built in one act on 2026-10-27 from the two searches; it carries no per-entry date, age, decay state, or history field at all (keys are only recipe/outcome/narrowing/grade/status/windows). Standings frozen: 388 entries, 63 at the top grade (status promoted: 6 stock-direction + 57 movement), 325 on probation (grade B).
- Grading runs on record: two searches, both generated 2026-10-27 (first 06:06, split 2023-01-01, 6,385 names, 15,135 cells, 1,217 promoted at grade B; second 16:34, split 2025-07-01, 3,337 names, 21,345 cells). They are two datasets/splits graded the same day, not two grading dates of the book over time. The second search is a one-time replication of the first, and its outcome is already baked into the book's single standing and already published by the Grade-A Board: of the 1,217 first-search promoted cells, 964 were testable in the second search: 63 earned the top grade, 337 held up (confirmed, stay on probation in the book), 0 flipped, 564 did not reproduce, 253 untestable.
- Entries carrying a standing at more than one date: 0 of 388. The two dates inside 331 stock-direction entries are measurement window starts (January 1, 2023 and July 1, 2025), not grading dates; the 57 movement entries carry no windows at all. No dated promotion, demotion, or probation entry/exit events exist anywhere in the record: no later book version (promoted-book-1 is the only one), and no recorded output from the factory's decay machinery (its status overlay and forward grades files do not exist in the factory tree; the machinery is code only, never run on record).
- Reconciliation: the Router's stamp 2026-10-28T12:52:03.240595+00:00 is NOT a grading of the book. It is the grading-record time for the published Board and index cards, carried through the research wire into the Router page; it postdates and does not re-grade the frozen book. The Promoted Book's frozen standings (63 top, 325 probation) recount exactly from the frozen file.
- Honest page today: standings have been graded once so far, on October 27, 2026 (the two searches that day, stated as the grading dates that exist); movements recorded so far: none (no entry has changed standing since the book was frozen; promotions 0, demotions 0, probation entries/exits 0 as recorded events, because there is no second grading). No percentages (every movement group is under 30, and in fact zero). The watch starts from the October 27, 2026 grading; the one-time first-to-second-search replication outcome belongs to the Grade-A Board and must not be republished here as a decay trend.
