## Tape Heatmap (tool 55) — INSPECT 2026-10-28
- Design job (LINEUP-DESIGN-SLATE-THREE-2026-10-28.md, tool 55, TapeBoard class), stated exactly: "the market as a heatmap: sectors and large names colored by flow and positioning readings, one screen, no tables." Backing status in the design: dissection only, 0.2 percent backed; backing to create = per-cell source definitions frozen in writing, each cell's reading reproducible from a named input, whole board graded as a cohort. NOTE: the build brief's strike-by-expiry grid over the tape record is a narrower view than the design's sector/name board; the grid is what the record can actually support (below), and the parent should reconcile the two shapes before build.
- Source of record: morgue/output/tape_card.json (written by morgue/tape_card.py), cross-checked by site/refresh_flow_tape.py against morgue/output/morgue-feed.json and morgue/output/ledger.jsonl; published as site/flow-tape/data.json. Session totals published by site/activity-profile/data.json (refresh_activity.py) from the same card.
- FRESHEST SESSION ON RECORD: 2026-10-27 (published 2026-10-27T20:46:23Z). Activity Profile holds 2 sessions (2026-10-26 and 2026-10-27); the tape card and Flow Tape data carry only 2026-10-27.
- FULL PRINTS DO NOT SURVIVE: the tape pipeline examines 990,555 prints for 2026-10-27 (SPY 476,456; QQQ 366,373; IWM 147,726) but persists ONLY per-name aggregates plus the 25 largest alerts per name. morgue/tape_card.py line 175 truncates agg["alerts"] = agg["alerts"][:25] and writes nothing else print-level; no print-level file exists in morgue/output or morgue/data_cache. The ledger holds the same material (tape-aggregate rows + tape-alert rows, 225 alerts = 3 runs x 75). So per name, individual prints with strike, expiry, side and premium survive ONLY for the 25 alert prints per name (75 total for the session); everything else is aggregates only. No other names beyond SPY, QQQ, IWM exist on this record at all.
- Each surviving alert print carries: timestamp, OCC contract code (strike, expiry, call/put parsed from it), price per contract, size (contracts), premium USD, heat score, side (ask/above_ask/bid/below_bid/mid/unknown), direction, golden-sweep and unusual flags. That is enough for a strike-by-expiry grid OVER THE 25 LARGEST ALERTS ONLY, per name.
- Grid shape if built from the alerts: SPY 25 alerts fall in 14 distinct strike/expiry/kind cells (13 strikes, 6 expiries: 2026-10-27, 10-30, 11-03, 11-04, 11-20, 12-04); QQQ 25 alerts in 18 cells (17 strikes, 7 expiries); IWM 25 alerts in 18 cells (17 strikes, 5 expiries). Empty cells everywhere else mean "no alert print on record there", never "no activity": the record examined ~1M prints it did not keep.
- RECONCILIATION (critical): a strike-by-expiry sum over the surviving alerts does NOT and must NOT be expected to equal the Activity Profile session totals. Alert premium sums: SPY $27.5M of the published $637,956,311 total (4.32%); QQQ $39.9M of $822,668,336 (4.85%); IWM $12.4M of $128,595,674 (9.68%). The grid's honest reconciliation target is the sum of the 25 published alerts per name (and the Flow Tape's own counts: 75 alerts published, 990,555 prints looked at), NOT the Activity Profile totals, which count every print examined including the ~99% not retained. Any build must say on its face that the grid shows only the session's largest alert prints and that its cells add up to the alert sum, not the session total. Per the brief's own rule, this is the honest shape for what the record supports: a largest-alerts heatmap, not a full-tape heatmap.
- COMPLETENESS DECLARATION the record carries (identical in tape card, Flow Tape data.json per group, and Activity Profile per name and per session): state "incomplete" for all three names for 2026-10-27: SPY 3 batches capped (476,456 prints examined, 3,467 contracts in scope), QQQ 2 capped (366,373; 3,294), IWM 1 capped (147,726; 1,398); reason in plain words: very busy contract groups hit the reading limit, so some prints in those groups may be missing. Session-level: 6 batches capped, 990,555 prints, 8,159 contracts in scope. The heatmap must carry this declaration; no name is silently complete.
- Verdict: full strike-by-expiry grid over all prints NOT supportable for any name (prints not retained). Largest-alerts strike-by-expiry grid supportable for all three names (SPY, QQQ, IWM), with the alert-sum reconciliation and the incomplete declaration on the face. Nothing changed except this checkpoint.

## Echo Board (tool 58) — INSPECT 2026-10-28
- Design job (LINEUP-DESIGN-SLATE-THREE-2026-10-28.md, tool 58, TradeEcho class), stated exactly: "tracked public traders' calls, echoed with timestamps and graded against the market from the moment of the call." Backing status: dissection only, 0.2 percent backed; backing to create = call-capture integrity first (a call counts only if captured before its outcome could be known, hash-timestamped at capture), then per-trader records on the ledger; a trader's card shows their full graded record or does not appear.
- SHAPE DIFFERENCE (material): the design's shape is a tracked-trader-calls board with timestamps and market grading. The brief's shape is an agreement-only board over the Second Opinion's families. The RECORD supports the brief's board only in the narrowed coverage shape below, and does NOT support the design's trader-calls board at all (no tracked trader calls, timestamps, or per-trader grading exist in the Second Opinion or Replication Wire records read). Build per the brief, reconciled as an agreement view over the Second Opinion record, never as trader calls.
- Source of record: site/second-opinion/data.json (md5 a714b7c300791e7d60824858f1e87305), session 2026-10-27, board published 2026-10-28T12:48:20Z, filings as of 2026-10-26. Six families are listed (price and trading history; options prints; modelled options positioning; filings and positioning; company facts; news and events), but company facts and news are silent for every name (the record's own silent reasons: no facts stored yet, no per-name news record kept), so at most four families can ever speak, and in practice at most three for any name.
- Coverage counts exactly as published and recomputed from families_speaking: 1,838 names on record; 3 names at 3 families, 38 at 2 families, 1,792 at 1 family, 5 at 0 families (the 5 have too little price history to score; 1,833 scored in all). Filings readings are on file for 50 names; prints and positioning cover only the 3 index funds SPY, QQQ, IWM.
- PER-FAMILY DIRECTION — THE DECISIVE LIMIT: the record carries a per-family direction word (stance: positive / mixed / cautious) only for price, prints, and positioning. The filings family carries NO stance and NO direction word: only numeric readings (named readings such as company insider activity, big-holder ownership, short positioning, with values), plus insider transaction counts/dates and short-interest figures where present. The per-name "directional" list therefore contains one entry per family THAT CARRIES A DIRECTION, not one per speaking family (the Second Opinion page's own wording is "families that carry a direction").
- The 38 two-family names are all price + filings, so each carries exactly ONE directional entry, equal to its price stance in every case (verified 38 of 38): 10 positive (ACN, AMN, CART, ELF, GO, LW, NWE, PR, RNG, URBN) and 28 mixed (ALRM, ANF, APA, CBOE, CBRL, CCI, CENTA, CHRD, EGBN, EL, FTRE, GKOS, HAS, HSTM, MAT, MTD, PCRX, PTEN, QLYS, QTWO, RGA, SM, TALO, TENB, UFCS, USO, VRNS, WAY). These are NOT two families pointing the same way on this record: only one family carries a direction, so no cross-family agreement can be claimed or shown for them. They are counted as two-family coverage, not listed as agreement.
- True agreement names: exactly 3, the index funds, each with 3 directional entries, all the same word "cautious": SPY (price cautious, score 56.5, close 769.66, 2 of 8 checks confirming, not qualified; prints cautious, 476,456 prints, net premium -264,674,842, call net -136,023,019, put net -128,651,823; positioning cautious, spot 771.65, flip 773.37, below the flip, call wall 780, put wall 750); QQQ (price cautious, 45.4, close 721.25, 1 of 8; prints cautious, 366,373 prints, net -236,447,838, call -155,977,406, put -80,470,432; positioning cautious, spot 723.47, flip 727.83, below the flip, call wall 720, put wall 700); IWM (price cautious, 47.6, close 280.18, 1 of 8; prints cautious, 147,726 prints, net -52,945,287, call -32,716,957, put -20,228,330; positioning cautious, spot 280.2, flip 288.45, below the flip, call wall 280, put wall 270). "Same way" in the record's own fields means: every entry in the name's directional list is the identical stance word (the Second Opinion's own agreement test: one distinct kind in directional = agreement; more than one = disagreement). Among all 41 names at 2-or-more speaking families, the only names with 2-or-more directional entries are these 3, and all 3 agree. No multi-direction name disagrees on this record.
- Replication Wire grading of the agreement claim (site/replication-wire/data.json, md5 d0af494cec58b577c8e055719587cb9f, 27 claims): the entry "A chart ranker's 0.736 ranking agreement is a forecasting result" (maker: the ChartsLector chart-ranking lane) is graded "Did not hold up": "Ruled not a prediction: the 0.736 measures agreement with one fitted 55-name vendor snapshot, not agreement with future returns, and it was reached only after three rotation mistakes and a split-adjustment fault in the ranker's data were corrected." Sample: one fitted snapshot of 55 names. The Echo Board must honour this: agreement only, never converted into a forecast, score, or direction call of its own.
- Verdict: the brief's agreement board is supportable ONLY as: 3 agreement names (SPY, QQQ, IWM, all cautious, with each family's own words/figures), plus counted-not-listed coverage (38 names covered by 2 families where only price carries a direction, 1,792 covered by 1 family, 5 by none). Any board listing the 38 as agreeing families would invent a filings direction the record does not carry. Nothing changed except this checkpoint.

## 2026-10-28 — Tool 59 Flow Engine: build phase done
- Built site/flow-engine/index.html (single self-contained page, inline CSS/JS, no external requests), site/refresh_flowengine.py, site/flow-engine/data.json; hub site/index.html now links the Flow Engine under "Ready to use" (href="flow-engine/") with wording matching the real state. - Ground truth, from the files read: the rule is the Flow Tape's printed rule (eight heat components with exact point bands; high heat at a score reaching 15; golden sweep = sweep + size at least the whole open interest + at least $1M in it; unusual tag = strike at least 10% away and expiry within 35 days; only the 25 largest alerts per index published). The record carries NO component-by-component breakdown for any alert, only the total heat score, so every worked example shows the figures the record carries and states plainly that the split is not broken out on record; no score is recomputed and the rule is never graded (the record's own grading note: alerts not yet graded). Proving state label from the Proving Ground's vocabulary: "Still being proven" (its entry for the Flow Tape is in that state).

## 2026-10-28 — Tool 64 Reversal Wire: inspect phase done
- Design job (Slate Four, tool 64): "reversal buy and sell signals as a timestamped wire, every signal kept and graded" (GainzAlgo class), backing = frozen signal definition + 30-session minimum cohort per regime before any signal appears without proving banner. Record actually supports labels only, no directions/signals: Scanner refresh states "Nothing ... that points anywhere is published: no direction word, no trigger, target, or level ... Labels only" and "claims no edge, makes no forecast, and gives no direction or suggestion." So wire entries must be worded as dated pattern labels, never buy/sell signals.
- Reversal-shaped kinds in the record's own vocabulary (strat-scanner/data.json words + per-name fields, freshest session only): hammer shape (body in top third of day's range), shooter shape (body in bottom third), failed 2-up ("2u red: failed 2u, potential 3": took prior high, closed below open) and failed 2-down ("2d green: failed 2d, potential 3": took prior low, closed above open). Strat Combos Board data carries NO hammer/shooter/failed fields at all (0 hits); it only groups the same Scanner labels into 397 sequence combinations and 8 completed-pattern groups.
- Counts, freshest session 2026-10-23 ONLY: hammer 129 entries / 129 names; shooter 62 / 62; failed 2-down 263 / 263; failed 2-up 173 / 173; failed total 436 (data.json failed_2_count). Tag entries total 627 across 561 distinct names (125 shape-only, 370 failed-only, 66 both shape and failed; shape and failed are separate tags on the same name/bar, not mutually exclusive). Universe: 1,817 labelled of 1,833 on record, 16 no bar.
- Earlier sessions: record DOES carry per-name plain labels (1 / 2u / 2d / 3 only) for the last 5 sessions 2026-10-19, 20, 21, 22, 23 (1,817 names each session; per-session label counts in inspect evidence), via sequence[] {date,label}. It carries NO shape, failed_2, or completed data for those earlier sessions: shape/failed_2/completed are computed only from classified[-1] / completed at last index in refresh_stratscanner.py. Therefore the wire cannot date hammer/shooter/failed entries to 2026-10-19 to 22; it carries exactly 1 session (2026-10-23) for those kinds and must say so (1 of the 5 sequence sessions), dating every entry 2026-10-23.
- Completed patterns (freshest only, for context, not counted above): 444 patterns across 328 names in Scanner and Combos completed_groups (same 8 patterns); of those, "2-2 reversal" patterns are 273 entries / 273 names (2u-2d 174 + 2d-2u 99). If the build intends "failed patterns" to include completed reversals, that is a distinct kind and must be named separately; the record's "failed" vocabulary is failed_2 only.

## 2026-10-28 — Tool 66 Breakout Scanner: inspect phase done
- Job exactly as designed (LINEUP-DESIGN-SLATE-FOUR-2026-10-28.md, tool 66): "the torn-down momentum method as a scanner face, not a sleeve: episodic-pivot and breakout candidates near highs with tightness measures, published as candidates with their follow-through graded." Note in the design: "this is a reading tool over the method's published rules. The sleeve freeze is untouched; nothing here trades." Backing to create in the design: setup definitions frozen from the teardown with worked examples as fixtures, and a candidates cohort graded on follow-through. The build task narrows the wording: description only, no name worded as a candidate to buy, no follow-through or performance claim (no graded record exists; the Replication Wire's bull-flag/breakout replications did not hold up), page carries its proving state instead.
- Input grounded in site/ai-chart-read/data.json (14M, read directly): top-level dates = shared calendar of 399 sessions, 2025-03-25 to 2026-10-23; bars = 1,833 names; each bars[name] = [start_index_into_dates, closes[], highs[], lows[]] with the three arrays equal length, no null closes, each name a continuous run of the calendar (source refresh_aichartread.py drops the source open, keeps close/high/low). session/history_to = 2026-10-23, scored_run_session = 2026-10-27 is a different field and not the price session. All three scanner figures are close-only (highest close, range of closes, rise), so closes alone suffice; highs/lows/labels are not needed.
- Own copy required: every built tool fetches a relative "data.json" in its own directory (no tool fetches another tool's file), so Breakout Scanner must ship its own trimmed data.json (dates + per-name start offset + closes) from its own refresh script over the same underlying history, not fetch ai-chart-read's file and not duplicate its labels/highs/lows.
- Window support, counted from the shipped bars: per-name sessions range 58 to 399 (1,807 names start at offset 0; 25 distinct start offsets). At least 252 sessions: 1,814 names. At least 21: all 1,833. At least 10: all 1,833 (minimum is 58, so tightness-10 and the 21-session rise are computable for every name). Shorter than 252: 19 names (58 to 248 sessions: ADIG 58, MBGL 81, MFP 83, HONA 92, FDXF 102, MAG 112, WDRN 113, AVEX 132, DRAM 142, VSNT 216, CYBR 222, VSOL 235, LITM 244, Q 245, DRNZ 248, plus MARS 162, SHAZ 173, CCXI 179, VGNT 143). For those, no 252-session high exists: the page must compute the high over the sessions actually on record and name that real window per name (never call it a 52-week or 252-session high), following the task's "named by its real window" rule.
- Freshest session is per name, not uniform: 1,817 names end 2026-10-23; 16 names end earlier (the exact same 16 that carry no pattern label): MAG 2025-09-03, CYBR 2026-02-10, LITM 2026-03-13, BITF 2026-04-02, CWEN.A 2026-04-30, CTRA 2026-05-06, APLS 2026-05-13, EA 2026-08-04, LEG 2026-08-26, CRNX 2026-08-31, HLX 2026-09-01, QRVO 2026-10-02, BLFS/PSKY/WBD 2026-10-05, ACA 2026-10-22. Their figures are as of their own last session (dates[start+len-1]); the page must date each such name by its own last session, never present it as a 2026-10-23 reading.
- Open for build: freeze the exact 21-session rise definition in print (last close vs the close 21 sessions earlier needs 22 closes; vs the first of the last 21 closes is 20 intervals) — all names satisfy either, but the printed definition must match the code.

- Refresh fails closed on any disagreement (card vs full list, history names vs scored names, any close vs the AI Chart Read's published closes, a non-continuous calendar run, window-support counts). ## Echo Lens (tool 69) — INSPECT 2026-10-28
- Design job (LINEUP-DESIGN-SLATE-FOUR-2026-10-28.md, tool 69, EchoAnalysis method class), stated exactly: "one analyst's published newsletter method as a chart lens: the method's own levels, reads, and conditions overlaid on any ticker." Backing status: "the method was coded as an algorithm in desk work in October and used as a lens in a retired tool; as a public tool it is unbacked." Backing to create: "the method's rules restated from the teardown with the author's own published examples as fixtures, and the lens's calls graded forward like any other source on the Echo Board's standard."
- SHAPE DIFFERENCE (material, stop signal for the build brief as written): the brief says the lens's method is "the tracked newsletter method whose per-name readings the Second Opinion's record carries as one of its families," assembled from site/second-opinion/data.json. THE RECORD DOES NOT SUPPORT THAT IDENTIFICATION. The Second Opinion's published record (site/second-opinion/data.json, md5 a714b7c300791e7d60824858f1e87305, state published, session 2026-10-27, board published 2026-10-28T12:48:20Z) carries exactly six families, none of them a newsletter method and none of them the EchoAnalysis method: (1) "Price and trading history" — per-name {close, score of 100, confirming, checks_total 8, dc_pct, qualified, stance positive/mixed/cautious}, plus a categories list on the 50 board-card names only; 1,833 names carry a reading, 5 carry none (ALTM, FREY, GOEV, SMAR, SQ, too little data). Its source is the scored trading day record (the rebuilt confluence board), not any newsletter. (2) "Options prints" — 3 names only (SPY, QQQ, IWM): {prints, total_premium, net_premium, call_net_premium, put_net_premium, high_heat, mean_heat, golden_sweeps, stance}. (3) "Modelled options positioning" — same 3 names: {spot, flip, below_flip, call_wall, put_wall, net_gex_b, chain_total, contracts_used, dix_pct, dix_date, stance}. (4) "Filings and positioning" — readings on file for 50 names dated 2026-10-26 (filings_as_of), per-name field present on 38 names: {readings (named readings of 100), insider_transactions + insider_latest on 34, si_pct + days_to_cover + si_settlement on 20}; carries NO stance and NO direction. (5) "Company facts" — silent for every name ("No company's facts are stored yet, so this family has nothing to read for any name."). (6) "News and events" — silent for every name ("No news or event record is kept per name, so this family has nothing to read for any name."). Coverage census as published and recomputed: 1,838 names on record; 3 at 3 families, 38 at 2, 1,792 at 1, 5 at 0; 41 names in all carry two or more families. Freshest session in the record: 2026-10-27 (filings dated 2026-10-26). A sweep of every other site data.json found no EchoAnalysis per-name readings anywhere: every "echo" hit is the ticker ECHO or the vendor name TradeEcho in the big-bundle claim.
- Replication Wire (site/replication-wire/data.json, md5 d0af494cec58b577c8e055719587cb9f, 27 claims, grading run 2026-10-28T12:52:03Z) has NO entry for the EchoAnalysis newsletter method. The ONLY entry whose headline figure is ruled an agreement measure rather than a forecast is a different method: claim "A chart ranker's 0.736 ranking agreement is a forecasting result.", maker "The ChartsLector chart-ranking lane", verdict_words "Did not hold up" (verdict did_not), result verbatim: "Ruled not a prediction: the 0.736 measures agreement with one fitted 55-name vendor snapshot, not agreement with future returns, and it was reached only after three rotation mistakes and a split-adjustment fault in the ranker's data were corrected." Sample: "One fitted snapshot of 55 names from the vendor". Study: "An audit of the ranker and its data, inside our research analysis". When: "Audit finished October 2026". That ruling already rides on the Echo Board (tool 58) page.
- What a lens page CAN truthfully show from this record, per name, with nothing blended: exactly one family's reading in that family's own published words/fields, for exactly the names that family covers, with the real coverage count on the page's face (price 1,833 of 1,838; prints 3; positioning 3; filings 38 with readings on file for 50; facts 0; news 0). No family in the record is the newsletter method the design names, so a page assembled from the Second Opinion's published data cannot present any family's reading as that method's reading without inventing an attribution the record does not carry. If the build proceeds under the brief's agreement-not-forecast discipline, the only ruling available to ride on the page is the ChartsLector 0.736 ruling quoted above, and it belongs to the chart-ranking lane, not to the EchoAnalysis method.
- Verdict: PARTIAL ground truth. Tool 69's job, the Second Opinion's six families with exact shapes/coverage/session, and the sole agreement-not-forecast ruling are all established from the files read. OPEN: the brief's family identification is contradicted by the record (no newsletter-method family exists in the Second Opinion's published data; no Replication Wire entry exists for the newsletter method). The build phase needs a ruling on which single family's readings the lens shows, or a different source of record for the method's own per-name readings, before anything is assembled. Nothing changed except this checkpoint.
